# www.founderspodcast.com: source 261

Reviewed 2026-10-24, Europe/Warsaw. Source: [original page](https://www.founderspodcast.com/). Tracking parameters are not part of source identity.

**Intake completed for recovered material.** 434 distinct catalog works linked; 0 extracted candidates remain unassessed. Raw source evidence and extraction records are retained under [the intake directory](../data/intake_shelves_261024/). Coverage limits, where present, are stated below.

The user requested reuse of existing assessments. Existing hard exclusions and vetoes are retained. Existing scored books receive only distinct-source REL recalculation; repeated titles, comments, episodes and editions within this source count once. No repeated mention is treated as independent evidence of quality. New judgments have low confidence unless stated otherwise.

Captured462episode pages and audited additional book links throughout their notes. All episodes form one source ID261. Author-store links are not book nominations. The SpaceX episode links an essay and announces an unreleased book; publication was not established, so it contributes no catalog book. The Koenigsegg episode is based on a documentary and interviews. Compilation episodes reuse the same book identities.

<a id="shelf-0709cd248c9a"></a>
### The 38 Letters from J.D. Rockefeller to His Son — Unknown author; falsely or uncertainly attributed to John D. Rockefeller

Status: **vetoed**. Sources: 261.

The purported correspondence cannot serve as reliable historical evidence. The reporting journalist records the archive's inability to locate matching letters and concrete anachronisms, while the named publisher denied involvement. Counterfactual evaluation retains those flaws. The 2021 compilation is an earliest identified edition, not a verified composition date; publication-year confidence is low. Evidence: https://www.linkedin.com/posts/robertfrank-cnbc_books-wealth-investing-activity-7290097147948015616-5rn9 ; https://financiallevel.com/2025/01/28/mystery-around-top-ranked-rockefeller-book-grows-as-university-publisher-denies-involvement/

Veto V1; confidence high. Attribution is materially unreliable: the Rockefeller Archive Center could not match the letters to its holdings and identified factual inconsistencies, including a claimed 1902 letter using the later Citibank name. OpenStax denied being the publisher. These defects undermine the work as purported authentic correspondence, regardless of the usefulness of individual advice. Overall and density confidence: **low**; no full-text density audit is claimed.
